The paper "InterChat: Enhancing Generative Visual Analytics using Multimodal Interactions" introduces a novel approach to generative visual analytics by incorporating multimodal interactions (specifically natural language and direct manipulation) into LLM-driven systems. The paper addresses the limitations of relying solely on natural language for analytical intent specification, which can be inefficient and error-prone. InterChat allows users to interact directly with visualizations through clicking, dragging, selecting, and even freehand sketching, while simultaneously using natural language to refine their analytical intent. The system is built on a multi-agent LLM architecture with specialized agents for manipulation descriptor generation, contextual interaction linking, and visualization generation using D3.js.  The authors evaluate InterChat through usage scenarios, a user study, and expert feedback, demonstrating improvements in accuracy and efficiency for complex analytical tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of generative visual analytics by directly addressing the limitations of language-only interaction. Combining natural language with direct manipulation offers a more intuitive and potentially more efficient way to express analytical intents. The design space exploration, the multi-agent architecture, and the integration of direct manipulation with D3.js code generation are valuable components.

*   **Significance:** Enhancing visual analytics with multimodal interaction has significant implications for usability and accessibility, particularly for users with varying technical expertise. By allowing users to combine language with direct actions on the visualisations, InterChat lowers the cognitive overhead involved in complex queries and reduces the number of iterations to get the desired result. The user study and expert feedback provides evidence that multimodal interactions can improve both efficiency and accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clearly Defined Problem:** The paper identifies a real problem within the emerging area of generative visual analytics and proposes a well-defined solution.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The combination of usage scenarios, a user study, and expert feedback strengthens the validity of the results and provides insights into the effectiveness of InterChat in diverse settings.
    *   **Well-Designed System:** The system architecture and the integration of different modalities appear well-engineered.
    *   **Design Space Exploration:** The paper includes a thorough exploration of the design space of multimodal interaction for generative visual analytics which is useful for the community.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written**: Overall, the paper is well written, clearly explaining the system, and providing convincing evaluations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM Dependency:** Like most LLM-driven research, the system's performance is inherently limited by the capabilities and limitations of the underlying LLMs used for intent inference and code generation. Specific error types (hallucinations, code errors) are acknowledged but are difficult to eliminate completely.
    *   **D3.js as Code Generation Target:** While D3.js offers flexibility, generating D3.js code can be complex and challenging for LLMs. The decision could contribute to code generation failures or introduce unnecessary complexity. Exploring alternative declarative visual languages may allow for better code generation in the future.
    *   **Modality Limitations:** The current implementation is limited to natural language and 2D direct manipulation. Future directions could explore additional modalities such as voice, gesture, or even eye-tracking for more expressive interactions.
    *   **Limited Computational Capabilities:** The current system only supports simple data transformations and aggregation. Supporting more complex statistical and computational tasks is a natural direction for future work.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work is likely to influence future research in multimodal visual analytics and LLM-based visualization systems. It provides a valuable blueprint for integrating direct manipulation into generative visual analytics. It also highlights the design considerations and benefits of a multimodal approach, encouraging the exploration of new modalities and interaction techniques.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper addresses a significant problem, offers a well-designed and evaluated solution, and has the potential to influence future research. The limitations stemming from LLM dependencies are acknowledged, and the exploration of the design space is comprehensive. While there is room for improvement in areas like expanding interaction modalities and incorporating more sophisticated data transformations, the paper presents a solid contribution that significantly advances the field of generative visual analytics. The work shows potential to impact data analysis practices, make it more intuitive and accessible. Because of the solid contribution and clear limitations, I believe the paper warrants an 8 out of 10.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Simple Self Organizing Map with Visual Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04121v1)
- **Authors**: Alan Luo, Kaiwen Yuan
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have demonstrated exceptional performance in various vision tasks. However, they tend to underperform on smaller datasets due to their inherent lack of inductive biases. Current approaches address this limitation implicitly-often by pairing ViTs with pretext tasks or by distilling knowledge from convolutional neural networks (CNNs) to strengthen the prior. In contrast, Self-Organizing Maps (SOMs), a widely adopted self-supervised framework, are inherently structured to preserve topology and spatial organization, making them a promising candidate to directly address the limitations of ViTs in limited or small training datasets. Despite this potential, equipping SOMs with modern deep learning architectures remains largely unexplored. In this study, we conduct a novel exploration on how Vision Transformers (ViTs) and Self-Organizing Maps (SOMs) can empower each other, aiming to bridge this critical research gap. Our findings demonstrate that these architectures can synergistically enhance each other, leading to significantly improved performance in both unsupervised and supervised tasks. Code will be publicly available.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ViT-SOM, a novel framework that integrates Vision Transformers (ViTs) with Self-Organizing Maps (SOMs). It aims to address the limitations of ViTs when trained on small datasets (due to a lack of inherent inductive biases) and the feature abstraction limitations of traditional SOMs.  ViT-SOM uses the ViT to generate embeddings of input images and then uses a SOM to learn a topology-preserving representation of these embeddings.  The authors evaluate their approach on unsupervised clustering and supervised classification tasks using several benchmark datasets, demonstrating improved performance compared to baselines and other SOM-based methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of combining ViTs and SOMs to leverage their complementary strengths is moderately novel. While related works have explored CNN-SOM combinations, the ViT-SOM fusion represents a distinct exploration within the context of modern deep learning architectures. The use of cosine similarity as a distance metric within the SOM is a minor, but potentially beneficial, refinement.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies primarily in its demonstration of the potential for integrating SOMs with modern deep learning models. While ViTs have become dominant in vision, they can struggle on small datasets. SOMs, with their inherent ability to preserve topology, offer a valuable inductive bias. The results show performance improvements in both unsupervised and supervised settings, suggesting that ViT-SOM can be a viable approach for small-data learning scenarios. The paper fills a gap in research by exploring the mutually beneficial interactions between SOMs and ViTs, a topic that has been relatively underexplored in previous literature.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and experiments.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate consistent improvements over baselines and other SOM-based methods.
    *   The visualizations (UMAP projections and decoded prototypes) provide insights into the learned representations.
    *   The method is relatively simple to implement, increasing its potential for adoption by other researchers.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The ViT architecture used is a "tiny" version, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to larger, more complex ViTs.
    *   The comparisons are primarily to SOM-based methods.  While direct comparison to other small-data methods for ViTs would be even more compelling
    *   The paper lacks a detailed ablation study to analyze the specific contributions of the ViT and SOM components.  It would be beneficial to determine which part of the architecture provides the most value.
    *   The authors acknowledge a limitation regarding the sensitivity of SOMs to initialization and the fixed grid structure. Further exploration of adaptive grid structures could improve the robustness of the method.
    *   The experimental setting for unsupervised clustering and supervised classification tasks is separate and might not reflect real-world scenarios where models often benefit from pretraining.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in the following areas:

    *   Integration of SOMs with modern deep learning architectures.
    *   Development of new methods for small-data learning.
    *   Exploration of inductive biases for ViTs.
    *   Self-supervised learning and representation learning.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach that yields promising results. However, the limited scope of experiments (tiny ViT, specific datasets), the lack of a thorough ablation study, and the acknowledged limitations regarding SOMs' sensitivity constrain its overall impact. There is room for expansion to more complex experimental setting. The performance increase shown, especially on some datasets is impressive. It presents a clear combination of two approaches that could be useful. Taking all factors into account, a score of 6 is warranted.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Diff-Reg v2: Diffusion-Based Matching Matrix Estimation for Image Matching and 3D Registration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04127v1)
- **Authors**: Qianliang Wu, Haobo Jiang, Yaqing Ding, Lei Luo, Jin Xie, Jian Yang
- **Abstract**: Establishing reliable correspondences is crucial for all registration tasks, including 2D image registration, 3D point cloud registration, and 2D-3D image-to-point cloud registration. However, these tasks are often complicated by challenges such as scale inconsistencies, symmetry, and large deformations, which can lead to ambiguous matches. Previous feature-based and correspondence-based methods typically rely on geometric or semantic features to generate or polish initial potential correspondences. Some methods typically leverage specific geometric priors, such as topological preservation, to devise diverse and innovative strategies tailored to a given enhancement goal, which cannot be exhaustively enumerated. Additionally, many previous approaches rely on a single-step prediction head, which can struggle with local minima in complex matching scenarios. To address these challenges, we introduce an innovative paradigm that leverages a diffusion model in matrix space for robust matching matrix estimation. Our model treats correspondence estimation as a denoising diffusion process in the matching matrix space, gradually refining the intermediate matching matrix to the optimal one. Specifically, we apply the diffusion model in the doubly stochastic matrix space for 3D-3D and 2D-3D registration tasks. In the 2D image registration task, we deploy the diffusion model in a matrix subspace where dual-softmax projection regularization is applied. For all three registration tasks, we provide adaptive matching matrix embedding implementations tailored to the specific characteristics of each task while maintaining a consistent "match-to-warp" encoding pattern. Furthermore, we adopt a lightweight design for the denoising module. In inference, once points or image features are extracted and fixed, this module performs multi-step denoising predictions through reverse sampling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Diff-Reg v2, a novel approach for image matching and 3D registration that leverages a diffusion model within the matching matrix space. It addresses challenges like scale inconsistency, symmetry, and large deformations by treating correspondence estimation as a denoising process, iteratively refining the matching matrix. The model is adapted for 3D-3D, 2D-3D, and 2D image registration tasks using task-specific matching matrix embeddings and a lightweight denoising module. Evaluations on multiple datasets demonstrate the approach's effectiveness in improving registration accuracy compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in applying diffusion models directly to the matching matrix space, which is a departure from previous diffusion-based methods in registration that primarily operate in SE(3) space or on flow fields. This allows the incorporation of global constraints during the denoising process, potentially leading to more geometrically and semantically consistent correspondences. Introducing adaptive matching matrix embedding implementations based on specific characteristics to each registration task is also novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the robustness and accuracy of image matching and 3D registration, which are fundamental tasks in computer vision and robotics. By framing correspondence estimation as a diffusion process, the model can better handle noisy or ambiguous matches and escape local minima. The consistent implementation of the matrix encoding module for 3D and 2D tasks adds significant value.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel approach:**  The application of diffusion models to the matching matrix is innovative.
    *   **Adaptability:** The model's adaptability to different registration tasks (2D, 2D-3D, 3D) through task-specific embeddings is a strength.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents extensive experiments on diverse datasets.
    *   **Lightweight Design:** The efficient denoising module contributes to faster convergence.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** Diffusion models are computationally intensive. While the paper emphasizes a lightweight design, the reported running times are still higher than some existing methods. Future work needs to focus on acceleration of the sampling.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While empirically effective, a deeper theoretical analysis of the diffusion process in the matching matrix space could strengthen the paper. A more in-depth analysis on the convergence property during reverse processing.
    *   **Dataset dependency:**  The quantitative results shows the methods tend to perform well in indoor environments, with limitations when applied to outdoor environments.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in image matching and 3D registration by demonstrating the effectiveness of diffusion models in the matching matrix space. The task-specific embedding implementations and lightweight denoising module designs are also valuable contributions that may inspire other researchers.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    While the core idea of applying diffusion models to the matching matrix is novel, the model still needs further optimization.  The speed improvement should be improved. It successfully demonstrates empirical gains over state-of-the-art methods on several benchmarks.  There is a clear potential for future research building upon this foundation, specifically with further refinement of matrix encoding module and denoising sampling module.
    Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Token-Efficient Long Video Understanding for Multimodal LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04130v1)
- **Authors**: Jindong Jiang, Xiuyu Li, Zhijian Liu, Muyang Li, Guo Chen, Zhiqi Li, De-An Huang, Guilin Liu, Zhiding Yu, Kurt Keutzer, Sungjin Ahn, Jan Kautz, Hongxu Yin, Yao Lu, Song Han, Wonmin Byeon
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in video-based multimodal large language models (Video-LLMs) have significantly improved video understanding by processing videos as sequences of image frames. However, many existing methods treat frames independently in the vision backbone, lacking explicit temporal modeling, which limits their ability to capture dynamic patterns and efficiently handle long videos. To address these limitations, we introduce STORM (\textbf{S}patiotemporal \textbf{TO}ken \textbf{R}eduction for \textbf{M}ultimodal LLMs), a novel architecture incorporating a dedicated temporal encoder between the image encoder and the LLM. Our temporal encoder leverages the Mamba State Space Model to integrate temporal information into image tokens, generating enriched representations that preserve inter-frame dynamics across the entire video sequence. This enriched encoding not only enhances video reasoning capabilities but also enables effective token reduction strategies, including test-time sampling and training-based temporal and spatial pooling, substantially reducing computational demands on the LLM without sacrificing key temporal information. By integrating these techniques, our approach simultaneously reduces training and inference latency while improving performance, enabling efficient and robust video understanding over extended temporal contexts. Extensive evaluations show that STORM achieves state-of-the-art results across various long video understanding benchmarks (more than 5\% improvement on MLVU and LongVideoBench) while reducing the computation costs by up to $8\times$ and the decoding latency by 2.4-2.9$\times$ for the fixed numbers of input frames. Project page is available at https://research.nvidia.com/labs/lpr/storm
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STORM (Spatiotemporal Token Reduction for Multimodal LLMs), a novel architecture aimed at improving long video understanding with Video-LLMs. The core idea is to integrate a dedicated temporal encoder, based on the Mamba State Space Model, between the image encoder and the LLM. This encoder integrates temporal information into image tokens, creating enriched representations that capture inter-frame dynamics across the video sequence. By encoding temporal dynamics early, the architecture facilitates effective token reduction strategies (test-time sampling, training-based temporal and spatial pooling) reducing LLM computational burden without sacrificing key temporal information. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art results on several long video understanding benchmarks while reducing computation costs and decoding latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its architectural design choices of using Mamba SSM for temporal encoding within a Video-LLM and combining this with token reduction techniques. The core concepts of temporal modeling and token reduction aren't entirely new in the Video-LLM field; however, the specific combination and the use of Mamba to achieve both performance improvements *and* efficiency gains are novel. The test-time token sampling is a relatively simple strategy but its effectiveness within the STORM architecture contributes positively to the efficiency aspect.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to enable more efficient and robust Video-LLMs for handling long videos. Current methods often struggle with the computational demands of processing long sequences, leading to compromises in performance or scalability. STORM tackles this limitation by reducing the computational cost while maintaining, or even improving, performance on long video understanding tasks. This opens up possibilities for more complex video-based applications that require processing of extended temporal contexts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents extensive experimental results demonstrating significant performance improvements on various benchmarks while simultaneously reducing computational costs and latency.
    *   **Effective Architecture:** The proposed STORM architecture effectively addresses the limitations of existing Video-LLMs in handling long videos by explicitly modeling temporal dynamics and facilitating token reduction.
    *   **Computational Efficiency:** The paper showcases how STORM achieves significant reduction in computational costs and latency compared to existing methods, making it more practical for real-world applications.
    *   **Rigorous Ablation Studies:** The authors conduct thorough ablation studies to analyze the impact of different components of the STORM architecture and compression techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While significant in certain aspects, the improvements over the baseline models could be seen as incremental. The performance gains might not be revolutionary but still meaningful within the scope of this kind of research.
    *   **Complexity:** Incorporating the Mamba layer adds complexity to the overall architecture. While the authors address this with token reduction techniques, it could still present challenges for adoption and integration in certain environments.
    *   **Limited to Mamba SSM:** The framework hinges on Mamba. Future studies could experiment on other sequence modeling alternatives to evaluate their compatibility and comparative effectiveness.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper has the potential to influence the design of future Video-LLMs by demonstrating the effectiveness of explicit temporal modeling and token reduction for handling long videos.
    *   The use of Mamba SSM within a Video-LLM could inspire further research on applying this architecture to other multimodal tasks and domains.
    *   The efficiency gains achieved by STORM could facilitate the development of more practical and scalable video-based applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of 7 because the paper combines existing techniques (temporal modeling, token reduction) in a novel and effective way within the context of Video-LLMs. While the core concepts aren't groundbreaking, the implementation, the empirical results, and the potential impact on improving efficiency and scalability justify a solid score.  The thorough ablation studies and the clear presentation of results also strengthen the paper.  A higher score would be warranted if the performance gains were more dramatic or if the design provided a more radically new approach to the problem.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Biological Sequence with Language Model Prompting: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04135v1)
- **Authors**: Jiyue Jiang, Zikang Wang, Yuheng Shan, Heyan Chai, Jiayi Li, Zixian Ma, Xinrui Zhang, Yu Li
- **Abstract**: Large Language models (LLMs) have emerged as powerful tools for addressing challenges across diverse domains. Notably, recent studies have demonstrated that large language models significantly enhance the efficiency of biomolecular analysis and synthesis, attracting widespread attention from academics and medicine. In this paper, we systematically investigate the application of prompt-based methods with LLMs to biological sequences, including DNA, RNA, proteins, and drug discovery tasks. Specifically, we focus on how prompt engineering enables LLMs to tackle domain-specific problems, such as promoter sequence prediction, protein structure modeling, and drug-target binding affinity prediction, often with limited labeled data. Furthermore, our discussion highlights the transformative potential of prompting in bioinformatics while addressing key challenges such as data scarcity, multimodal fusion, and computational resource limitations. Our aim is for this paper to function both as a foundational primer for newcomers and a catalyst for continued innovation within this dynamic field of study.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Biological Sequence with Language Model Prompting: A Survey".

**Summary:**

The paper presents a survey of how large language models (LLMs) and prompt engineering are being applied to biological sequence analysis tasks.  It focuses on how prompts can adapt LLMs to DNA, RNA, protein, and drug discovery applications. The survey organizes and categorizes the different prompting methods, discusses the contributions of models like AlphaFold and the ESM series, and highlights the challenges (data scarcity, multimodal fusion, resource constraints) and future directions (data-centric methods, multi-modal prompting, efficient adaptation).  The paper aims to provide a foundation for researchers entering the field and to encourage further innovation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timeliness and Relevance:**  The survey addresses a very active and rapidly growing research area. The convergence of LLMs and bioinformatics is generating considerable interest, making a survey a valuable contribution.
    *   **Organization and Clarity:**  The paper attempts a systematic overview, categorizing different prompting applications across various biological sequence types. The inclusion of figures illustrating the application of prompt methods in DNA, RNA, protein and drug is helpful for understanding.
    *   **Identification of Challenges and Future Directions:** The survey effectively highlights the key obstacles facing the field, especially data scarcity and the need for multimodal integration. The identified future directions are realistic and relevant.
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a broad range of recent studies.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth of Technical Analysis:** While the paper describes various approaches, it doesn't delve into the nitty-gritty details of prompt design or the architectural nuances of the mentioned LLMs. This lack of technical depth limits its value for experienced researchers who need more than a high-level overview. Many methods are mentioned without fully explaining how each works and their performance advantages and disadvantages.
    *   **Incremental Novelty as a Survey:** A survey's value lies in its comprehensiveness and insightful analysis. While the paper is up-to-date, its analysis is descriptive rather than deeply critical. It doesn't present a unifying theoretical framework or offer a radical re-interpretation of the existing landscape.
    *   **Uneven focus on Prompting specific aspect:** The survey title suggests a strong focus on the "prompting" aspect of LLM-based biological sequence analysis, but the paper sometimes veers into discussing models and methods that use LLMs without a particularly innovative or significant prompting strategy. The emphasis can be slightly diluted.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper's primary significance is in providing a relatively up-to-date overview of the burgeoning field. This can be a useful entry point for newcomers. However, given the speed of advancement, a survey like this can become dated relatively quickly. The novelty lies in compiling these recent advancements in a structured manner, but the individual components are, of course, derived from existing published research.

*   **Impact:**

    The potential impact is moderate. The survey could serve as a resource for researchers starting in the field and could help to identify potential research gaps. However, its limited depth and analytical insights hinder its long-term impact on shaping the research agenda.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of **6**.

Here's why:

The survey is timely and well-organized, presenting a helpful overview of a rapidly evolving field. However, it lacks the depth of technical analysis and critical insight needed to truly push the field forward. The novelty is incremental, and the long-term impact may be limited by the lack of in-depth exploration of promising avenues or novel theoretical constructs. It does provide a needed service in gathering many recent studies together. Its main value is as a good introductory text and a relatively comprehensive list of citations. However, it falls short of providing a deeper critical assessment or a novel synthesis of the field.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Dynamic Benchmarking of Reasoning Capabilities in Code Large Language Models Under Data Contamination
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04149v1)
- **Authors**: Simin Chen, Pranav Pusarla, Baishakhi Ray
- **Abstract**: The rapid evolution of code largelanguage models underscores the need for effective and transparent benchmarking of their reasoning capabilities. However, the current benchmarking approach heavily depends on publicly available, human-created datasets. The widespread use of these fixed benchmark datasets makes the benchmarking process to be static and thus particularly susceptible to data contamination, an unavoidable consequence of the extensive data collection processes used to train Code LLMs. Existing approaches that address data contamination often suffer from human effort limitations and imbalanced problem complexity. To tackle these challenges, we propose \tool, a novel benchmarking suite for evaluating Code LLMs under potential data contamination. Given a seed programming problem, \tool employs multiple agents to extract and modify the context without altering the core logic, generating semantically equivalent variations. We introduce a dynamic data generation methods and conduct empirical studies on two seed datasets across 21 Code LLMs. Results show that \tool effectively benchmarks reasoning capabilities under contamination risks while generating diverse problem sets to ensure consistent and reliable evaluations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary of the paper followed by a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DyCodeEval, a novel dynamic benchmarking suite for evaluating code large language models (LLMs) under potential data contamination. DyCodeEval addresses the limitations of existing static benchmarks by generating semantically diverse yet complexity-controlled programming problems. It achieves this using LLM agents that extract and modify the context of seed problems without altering the core logic. The method involves scenario proposal, context generation, prompt rewriting, and validation.  The paper presents empirical results demonstrating that DyCodeEval can effectively benchmark reasoning capabilities even in the presence of data contamination, providing more reliable evaluations than static benchmarks. The generated problems also show strong diversity and maintain benchmarking stability. A new metric, DivPass, is proposed to leverage the dynamic nature of the benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to tackling a crucial problem in the evaluation of code LLMs: data contamination. The idea of dynamically generating benchmark problems using LLM agents, guided by metamorphic testing principles, is well-conceived and executed. Existing approaches often rely on manual effort or introduce unintended changes in problem complexity. DyCodeEval directly addresses these issues by explicitly separating algorithmic complexity from contextual details and ensuring the generated problems remain semantically equivalent. The proposed DivPass metric also shows promise in providing a more accurate assessment in the face of contamination.

**Significance:** Data contamination is a serious threat to the validity of LLM benchmarks. If models are inadvertently (or deliberately) trained on the benchmark datasets, their performance will be artificially inflated, masking their true capabilities. DyCodeEval offers a valuable tool for mitigating this risk and obtaining a more realistic picture of model performance. This is particularly important for researchers and practitioners who rely on benchmarks to track progress and compare different LLMs. By providing a dynamic and adaptable benchmarking framework, DyCodeEval contributes to a more robust and trustworthy evaluation process. The work can also inform the design of more effective data contamination detection/mitigation techniques.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined methodology:** The paper provides a clear and detailed explanation of the DyCodeEval methodology, including the roles of the different LLM agents and the validation process.
*   **Strong empirical results:** The experimental evaluation is thorough, covering a range of code LLMs and contamination scenarios. The results demonstrate the effectiveness of DyCodeEval in mitigating the impact of data contamination and providing more stable benchmarking results.
*   **Theoretical analysis:** The collision analysis provides a theoretical justification for the reduced risk of data contamination with DyCodeEval.
*   **Practical insights:** The findings regarding the impact of data contamination on static benchmarks and the limitations of existing mitigation strategies offer valuable insights for the community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational cost:** The paper acknowledges the computational cost of using LLMs to generate the benchmark problems. This could limit the scalability of DyCodeEval and make it less accessible to researchers with limited resources. The reliance on closed-source models such as CLAUDE adds another potential barrier.
*   **Dependence on LLM quality:** The quality of the generated benchmark problems depends on the quality of the LLM agents used in DyCodeEval. If these agents are not sufficiently capable, the generated problems may be inconsistent, poorly designed, or introduce unintentional biases. While the paper addresses this with a validation step, the risk of introducing noise into the evaluation process remains.
*   **Human verification**: The paper mentions the human verification process, and the rate is around 95%. However, there is no in-depth analysis of what causes that 5% inconsistency and what can be done to minimize this issue.
*   **Limited application scope**: While the paper focuses on code LLMs, the applicability of DyCodeEval to other types of LLMs or tasks is not explicitly discussed. Further research is needed to explore the generalizability of the approach. The work is mostly applied to code generation and not code understanding or refinement tasks.

**Overall:**

DyCodeEval is a significant contribution to the field of LLM evaluation. It offers a novel and effective approach to addressing the critical problem of data contamination, with strong theoretical and empirical support. While the computational cost and dependence on LLM quality are potential limitations, the benefits of DyCodeEval in providing more robust and trustworthy evaluations outweigh these drawbacks. The paper has the potential to influence the design of future benchmarks and evaluation methodologies.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**  The score reflects the strong novelty and significance of DyCodeEval, while acknowledging the limitations related to computational cost, reliance on LLM quality, and relatively specific application focus. The strengths of the paper justify a high score, but the weaknesses prevent it from reaching the highest levels of exceptional contributions. The score is above average but not groundbreaking.

One reason I have not scored this higher is, while the paper introduces a promising method to evaluate code LLMs dynamically, the novelty is somewhat incremental. Metamorphic testing has been previously explored in software testing and applying LLMs as agents is a growing trend. Furthermore, while the approach addresses data contamination effectively, the dependency on LLM qualities and the computational cost make it potentially expensive. Overall, while the idea and experimental results are promising, it might require further enhancement to address all the limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Ticktack : Long Span Temporal Alignment of Large Language Models Leveraging Sexagenary Cycle Time Expression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04150v1)
- **Authors**: Xue Han, Qian Hu, Yitong Wang, Wenchun Gao, Lianlian Zhang, Qing Wang, Lijun Mei, Chao Deng, Junlan Feng
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) suffer from temporal misalignment issues especially across long span of time. The issue arises from knowing that LLMs are trained on large amounts of data where temporal information is rather sparse over long times, such as thousands of years, resulting in insufficient learning or catastrophic forgetting by the LLMs. This paper proposes a methodology named "Ticktack" for addressing the LLM's long-time span misalignment in a yearly setting. Specifically, we first propose to utilize the sexagenary year expression instead of the Gregorian year expression employed by LLMs, achieving a more uniform distribution in yearly granularity. Then, we employ polar coordinates to model the sexagenary cycle of 60 terms and the year order within each term, with additional temporal encoding to ensure LLMs understand them. Finally, we present a temporal representational alignment approach for post-training LLMs that effectively distinguishes time points with relevant knowledge, hence improving performance on time-related tasks, particularly over a long period. We also create a long time span benchmark for evaluation. Experimental results prove the effectiveness of our proposal.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Ticktack: Long Span Temporal Alignment of Large Language Models Leveraging Sexagenary Cycle Time Expression" addresses the temporal misalignment issues in LLMs, particularly over long spans of time. The authors propose a method called "Ticktack" which uses the sexagenary cycle (a 60-year cycle) instead of Gregorian years for a more uniform distribution of temporal information. They then employ polar coordinates to model the sexagenary cycle and add temporal encoding to aid LLM understanding. Finally, they introduce a temporal representational alignment approach to post-train LLMs, improving performance on time-related tasks. The authors also create a new long time span benchmark called TempLS for evaluation. Experimental results across various open-source LLMs show improvements in time-related tasks using Ticktack.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using the sexagenary cycle is somewhat novel in the context of LLMs, although cyclical representations of time are not entirely new. The combination of the sexagenary cycle with polar coordinates and temporal encoding shows a level of innovation. The temporal alignment approach is also a meaningful contribution.
*   **Significance:** Temporal misalignment is a significant and well-recognized problem in LLMs, particularly given the increasing reliance on these models in knowledge-intensive applications. The paper directly tackles this problem, offering a potentially effective solution.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The approach is clearly explained and well-structured.
    *   The use of a new benchmark dataset (TempLS) is a significant plus, as it addresses the lack of suitable long-span temporal datasets.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate a noticeable improvement across different LLMs.
    *   Ablation study sheds light on contribution of each module.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experimental evaluation, while promising, is limited to the described tasks. It needs validation across wider array of datasets.
    *   The implementation details lack specifics. For instance, the exact composition of the "time-related texts" used for post-training is missing.
    *   Hyperparameter tuning details should be more specific
    *   The sexagenary cycle itself is specific to Chinese/Far Eastern cultures. While it works in this setting, generalizability may be limited.
    *   The visualization in Figure 6 and 7, albeit interesting, could benefit from more analysis and discussion on the *types* of sentences and representations are more effectively clustered and aligned.
*   **Potential Impact:** If validated further, the "Ticktack" method could be a valuable addition to the LLM toolkit, improving the accuracy and reliability of these models in time-sensitive applications. The TempLS dataset has the potential to serve as a standard benchmark for long-span temporal reasoning.
*   **Rigorousness:** The paper presents a solid methodology and experimental results, the analysis could be more in-depth, particularly regarding the limitations and cultural dependencies. The evaluation metrics are standard, which is good, but could benefit from the inclusion of more detailed error analysis.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in LLMs. The introduction of the sexagenary cycle and the creation of the TempLS dataset are valuable contributions. The experimental results are promising, but the scope of evaluation and level of analysis limits potential. In all, there is ample scope to improve the research direction. Hence, the score is assigned as following.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### KidneyTalk-open: No-code Deployment of a Private Large Language Model with Medical Documentation-Enhanced Knowledge Database for Kidney Disease
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04153v1)
- **Authors**: Yongchao Long, Chao Yang, Gongzheng Tang, Jinwei Wang, Zhun Sui, Yuxi Zhou, Shenda Hong, Luxia Zhang
- **Abstract**: Privacy-preserving medical decision support for kidney disease requires localized deployment of large language models (LLMs) while maintaining clinical reasoning capabilities. Current solutions face three challenges: 1) Cloud-based LLMs pose data security risks; 2) Local model deployment demands technical expertise; 3) General LLMs lack mechanisms to integrate medical knowledge. Retrieval-augmented systems also struggle with medical document processing and clinical usability. We developed KidneyTalk-open, a desktop system integrating three technical components: 1) No-code deployment of state-of-the-art (SOTA) open-source LLMs (such as DeepSeek-r1, Qwen2.5) via local inference engine; 2) Medical document processing pipeline combining context-aware chunking and intelligent filtering; 3) Adaptive Retrieval and Augmentation Pipeline (AddRep) employing agents collaboration for improving the recall rate of medical documents. A graphical interface was designed to enable clinicians to manage medical documents and conduct AI-powered consultations without technical expertise. Experimental validation on 1,455 challenging nephrology exam questions demonstrates AddRep's effectiveness: achieving 29.1% accuracy (+8.1% over baseline) with intelligent knowledge integration, while maintaining robustness through 4.9% rejection rate to suppress hallucinations. Comparative case studies with the mainstream products (AnythingLLM, Chatbox, GPT4ALL) demonstrate KidneyTalk-open's superior performance in real clinical query. KidneyTalk-open represents the first no-code medical LLM system enabling secure documentation-enhanced medical Q&A on desktop. Its designs establishes a new framework for privacy-sensitive clinical AI applications. The system significantly lowers technical barriers while improving evidence traceability, enabling more medical staff or patients to use SOTA open-source LLMs conveniently.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KidneyTalk-open, a desktop application designed to provide privacy-preserving medical decision support for kidney disease. It addresses the challenges of using Large Language Models (LLMs) in clinical settings, such as data security concerns with cloud-based LLMs, the technical expertise needed for local deployment, and the limitations of general LLMs in integrating medical knowledge. KidneyTalk-open overcomes these obstacles by integrating: (1) No-code deployment of open-source LLMs (like DeepSeek-r1, Qwen2.5) via a local inference engine; (2) A medical document processing pipeline for context-aware chunking and filtering; and (3) An Adaptive Retrieval and Augmentation Pipeline (AddRep) utilizing agent collaboration. The system enables clinicians to manage medical documents and obtain AI-powered consultations without specialized technical skills. The authors validate their approach using a Chinese Nephrology Medical Exam MCQ dataset and case studies. Results show that AddRep improves accuracy and suppresses hallucinations, while comparative studies indicate superior performance in real clinical queries compared to mainstream localized LLM applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely useful system in the medical domain by providing a no-code, privacy-preserving LLM-based solution. The innovative aspects include the combination of no-code deployment, optimized document processing and AddRep strategy. The most original aspect is providing a readily deployable medical Q&A system based on SOTA open-source LLMs for clinicians without technical expertise.

*   **Significance:** KidneyTalk-open offers a practical solution for medical institutions to leverage the benefits of LLMs without compromising patient privacy. The results demonstrate improvements in accuracy and robustness compared to baseline methods and other existing systems. The system can potentially empower medical staff and patients with better access to relevant knowledge and improve decision-making processes in kidney disease diagnosis and treatment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a critical need:** Privacy-preserving medical AI is essential, and the paper directly tackles this challenge.
    *   **Practicality:** The focus on no-code deployment significantly increases accessibility for clinicians.
    *   **Technical innovation:** The AddRep pipeline and the integrated architecture represent a well-engineered system.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The use of an MCQ dataset and real-world case studies provides a robust evaluation of the system's capabilities.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited generalization:** The evaluation is primarily based on Chinese nephrology guidelines and medical exam questions. Further validation with datasets and clinical scenarios from different regions and medical specialties would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Dependency on specific LLMs:** The system's performance relies on the selected open-source LLMs (DeepSeek-r1, Qwen2.5). The long-term sustainability of the system depends on the continued availability and updates of these LLMs.
    *   **Limited assessment of usability:** While the paper emphasizes no-code deployment, it would benefit from a formal usability study with clinicians to assess the ease of use and effectiveness of the graphical interface.
    *   **Limited discussion of computational resource requirements.** Despite focusing on local deployment, a rigorous assessment of the required computational resources (memory, CPU, GPU) for acceptable inference speeds on typical medical workstations could strengthen the findings.

*   **Potential influence:** KidneyTalk-open can serve as a template for developing similar privacy-preserving medical AI systems for other specialties. The open-source nature of the system can facilitate further research and development in this area. It has the potential to become a practical tool for improving healthcare accessibility and quality in resource-constrained environments.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the novelty and significance of the paper, along with its strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7 is appropriate. While the paper presents a valuable and well-engineered system, the limitations in generalization, dependence on specific LLMs, and usability assessment prevent it from achieving a higher score. The system's potential for real-world impact and the innovative combination of technologies justify a score above average.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Semantic Retrieval Augmented Contrastive Learning for Sequential Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04162v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqiang Cui, Yunpeng Weng, Xing Tang, Xiaokun Zhang, Dugang Liu, Shiwei Li, Peiyang Liu, Bowei He, Weihong Luo, Xiuqiang He, Chen Ma
- **Abstract**: Sequential recommendation aims to model user preferences based on historical behavior sequences, which is crucial for various online platforms. Data sparsity remains a significant challenge in this area as most users have limited interactions and many items receive little attention. To mitigate this issue, contrastive learning has been widely adopted. By constructing positive sample pairs from the data itself and maximizing their agreement in the embedding space,it can leverage available data more effectively. Constructing reasonable positive sample pairs is crucial for the success of contrastive learning. However, current approaches struggle to generate reliable positive pairs as they either rely on representations learned from inherently sparse collaborative signals or use random perturbations which introduce significant uncertainty. To address these limitations, we propose a novel approach named Semantic Retrieval Augmented Contrastive Learning (SRA-CL), which leverages semantic information to improve the reliability of contrastive samples. SRA-CL comprises two main components: (1) Cross-Sequence Contrastive Learning via User Semantic Retrieval, which utilizes large language models (LLMs) to understand diverse user preferences and retrieve semantically similar users to form reliable positive samples through a learnable sample synthesis method; and (2) Intra-Sequence Contrastive Learning via Item Semantic Retrieval, which employs LLMs to comprehend items and retrieve similar items to perform semantic-based item substitution, thereby creating semantically consistent augmented views for contrastive learning. SRA-CL is plug-and-play and can be integrated into standard sequential recommendation models. Extensive experiments on four public datasets demonstrate the effectiveness and generalizability of the proposed approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Semantic Retrieval Augmented Contrastive Learning for Sequential Recommendation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SRA-CL, a novel framework designed to improve sequential recommendation by addressing the data sparsity issue. SRA-CL leverages large language models (LLMs) to enhance contrastive learning. It consists of two main components: 1) Cross-Sequence Contrastive Learning via User Semantic Retrieval, which uses LLMs to understand user preferences and retrieve semantically similar users to form reliable positive samples; and 2) Intra-Sequence Contrastive Learning via Item Semantic Retrieval, which employs LLMs to comprehend items and retrieve similar items for semantic-based item substitution, creating consistent augmented views.  The framework is model-agnostic and can be integrated into standard sequential recommendation models. Extensive experiments on four datasets are conducted to demonstrate its effectiveness and generalizability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to enhance contrastive learning in sequential recommendation is relatively novel. The specific implementation, with its two-pronged approach (cross-sequence and intra-sequence retrieval augmented), provides a concrete methodology. While LLMs have been used in recommendation before, their application in this specific manner to improve *contrastive learning samples* is a distinguishable contribution.  However, the general trend of using LLMs for recommendation is quite active, so the novelty, while present, is not groundbreaking.

* **Significance:** The paper tackles a critical problem in sequential recommendation: data sparsity.  Contrastive learning has shown promise in addressing this, but generating reliable positive samples has been a challenge. Using LLMs to inject semantic information has the potential to greatly improve the quality of contrastive learning and, therefore, recommendation performance. The results presented in the paper support this potential. The plug-and-play nature of the approach is also a significant practical advantage.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing contrastive learning methods for sequential recommendation.
    * **Well-Defined Approach:**  SRA-CL is well-defined and explained, with clear descriptions of each component.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  The paper includes extensive experiments on multiple datasets, comparing against a broad range of baselines. Ablation studies provide further insight into the contribution of each module.
    * **Model-Agnostic Design:** The plug-and-play architecture facilitates easy adoption across different recommendation models.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on External APIs:** The reliance on public LLM APIs might limit reproducibility and scalability in certain settings (e.g., data privacy concerns, API costs). This dependence also means performance can be affected by the quality of the external API. While using DeepSeek-V3 is advantageous in some ways, it introduces external dependencies not generally found in other works.
    * **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The framework introduces several new hyperparameters (related to attention, weighting of loss terms, and the retrieval process). While a hyperparameter study is conducted, there could be settings that the authors didn't consider for other datasets, and which are important to the performance of the approach.
    * **Incremental Improvement:**  While the performance improvements are consistent, some may see them as incremental, given the complexity introduced by the LLM integration.  This depends, of course, on the deployment context and the value of improved recommendation accuracy.
    * **Compute cost:** The paper doesn't detail the extra compute costs associated with implementing and training SRA-CL.

* **Impact:**  The paper could have a noticeable impact on the field of sequential recommendation. The idea of leveraging LLMs to improve contrastive learning samples is applicable to other recommendation tasks as well. The plug-and-play architecture can encourage adoption and further research.  Follow-up work could explore different LLMs, fine-tuning strategies for the adapter layers, and applications to other recommendation domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of sequential recommendation. It addresses a practical problem (data sparsity) with a novel and well-executed approach that leverages the power of LLMs. The empirical results support the effectiveness of the framework, and the model-agnostic design is a significant advantage.

However, the reliance on external APIs, the potential hyperparameter sensitivity, and the incremental nature of the improvements (relative to the increased complexity), hold it back from receiving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TIMER: Temporal Instruction Modeling and Evaluation for Longitudinal Clinical Records
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04176v1)
- **Authors**: Hejie Cui, Alyssa Unell, Bowen Chen, Jason Alan Fries, Emily Alsentzer, Sanmi Koyejo, Nigam Shah
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have emerged as promising tools for assisting in medical tasks, yet processing Electronic Health Records (EHRs) presents unique challenges due to their longitudinal nature. While LLMs' capabilities to perform medical tasks continue to improve, their ability to reason over temporal dependencies across multiple patient visits and time frames remains unexplored. We introduce TIMER (Temporal Instruction Modeling and Evaluation for Longitudinal Clinical Records), a framework that incorporate instruction-response pairs grounding to different parts of a patient's record as a critical dimension in both instruction evaluation and tuning for longitudinal clinical records. We develop TIMER-Bench, the first time-aware benchmark that evaluates temporal reasoning capabilities over longitudinal EHRs, as well as TIMER-Instruct, an instruction-tuning methodology for LLMs to learn reasoning over time. We demonstrate that models fine-tuned with TIMER-Instruct improve performance by 7.3% on human-generated benchmarks and 9.2% on TIMER-Bench, indicating that temporal instruction-tuning improves model performance for reasoning over EHR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TIMER: Temporal Instruction Modeling and Evaluation for Longitudinal Clinical Records":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TIMER, a framework for evaluating and improving the temporal reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) when applied to longitudinal Electronic Health Records (EHRs). TIMER consists of two components: TIMER-Bench, a benchmark that evaluates temporal reasoning, and TIMER-Instruct, an instruction-tuning methodology that improves model performance. The paper highlights the limitations of existing EHR benchmarks in adequately capturing temporal complexity and recency bias. TIMER-Bench aims to address these limitations through temporal-aware distribution evaluation. TIMER-Instruct focuses on improving models' performance across various time points in a patient's record by instruction tuning with model-generated instruction-response pairs, controlling the temporal distribution of instructions during training. Experiments show that models fine-tuned with TIMER-Instruct demonstrate improved performance on both human-generated benchmarks and TIMER-Bench, indicating that temporal instruction tuning improves the LLM's ability to reason over EHR data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a significant contribution by identifying and addressing a critical gap in the evaluation and training of LLMs for processing longitudinal EHRs. The insight that temporal distribution of instructions is a crucial, yet often overlooked, factor is novel. While there's existing work on temporal reasoning in LLMs and instruction tuning for medical applications, the specific focus on addressing temporal biases and improving reasoning across the entire patient timeline is fairly new.
*   **Significance:** EHR data is inherently longitudinal. The ability to effectively reason over this data is essential for many healthcare applications, including chronic disease management, multi-visit care planning, and patient history synthesis. Improving LLMs' performance in these areas could have a substantial impact on clinical practice. The TIMER framework provides tools and methodologies that could push the field forward.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of inadequate temporal reasoning in existing benchmarks and the need for more comprehensive evaluation methods.
*   **Well-Designed Framework:** TIMER-Bench and TIMER-Instruct are well-designed components that address the identified limitations. The controlled distribution sampling in TIMER-Bench and the temporal-aware instruction tuning in TIMER-Instruct are thoughtfully constructed.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation is relatively thorough, comparing against several baselines and across multiple benchmarks (both human-generated and synthetic). The analysis of different temporal distribution strategies is also valuable.
*   **Reproducibility:** The commitment to releasing code and the dataset with appropriate usage agreements increases the reproducibility and impact of the work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Model-Generated Data:** While LLM-generated instruction-response pairs are scalable, there are potential concerns about the quality and biases present in this data. The paper makes an attempt to validate the model through a human study but it isn't enough to compensate for issues that arise with synthesized data.
*   **Limited Generalizability:** The experiments focus on data from a single academic medical center. It is possible that the approach may not be generalizable to EHRs from other institutions or healthcare systems.
*   **Complexity of LLM-Based Evaluation:** Relying on LLM-Judge for evaluation introduces another layer of complexity and potential bias. While the paper shows strong correlation with human ratings, it's essential to be aware of the limitations of this approach.
*   **Limited Clinical Validation:** The results demonstrate quantitative improvements in model performance, but more clinical validation is needed to assess the real-world impact of improved temporal reasoning. How do the improvements translate to better clinical decision making?

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could significantly influence how LLMs are evaluated and trained for healthcare applications. The focus on temporal reasoning and controlled distribution sampling could lead to the development of more robust and reliable models for processing EHR data. It also highlights the importance of addressing temporal biases in existing benchmarks and provides a valuable framework for future research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLMs for healthcare. The TIMER framework addresses a critical gap in temporal reasoning capabilities, offering valuable tools and methodologies for evaluation and instruction tuning. It provides a solid experimental evaluation to support its claims and intends to release its code. However, the reliance on model-generated data, limited generalizability, and the complexity of LLM-based evaluation temper the score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Measuring temporal effects of agent knowledge by date-controlled tool use
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04188v1)
- **Authors**: R. Patrick Xian, Qiming Cui, Stefan Bauer, Reza Abbasi-Asl
- **Abstract**: Temporal progression is an integral part of knowledge accumulation and update. Web search is frequently adopted as grounding for agent knowledge, yet its inappropriate configuration affects the quality of agent responses. Here, we construct a tool-based out-of-sample testing framework to measure the knowledge variability of large language model (LLM) agents from distinct date-controlled tools (DCTs). We demonstrate the temporal effects of an LLM agent as a writing assistant, which can use web search to help complete scientific publication abstracts. We show that temporal effects of the search engine translates into tool-dependent agent performance but can be alleviated with base model choice and explicit reasoning instructions such as chain-of-thought prompting. Our results indicate that agent evaluation should take a dynamical view and account for the temporal influence of tools and the updates of external resources.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the impact of time-dependent knowledge in large language model (LLM) agents, specifically focusing on how date-controlled tools (DCTs) affect their performance in scientific abstract completion. The authors construct a tool-based, out-of-sample (OOS) testing framework to measure the variability of LLM agents when using web search with date restrictions. They introduce the SciBreak dataset of scientific breakthroughs between 2000 and 2024 and investigate how the temporal effects of search engines translate into tool-dependent agent performance. The study demonstrates that base model choice and explicit reasoning instructions (like chain-of-thought prompting) can alleviate these temporal effects. The findings suggest that agent evaluation should consider the dynamic influence of tools and the updates of external resources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on evaluating LLM agents with *date-controlled* tools in a *scientific knowledge* domain. While the concept of evaluating LLMs in OOS scenarios and temporal reasoning is not entirely new, the authors' emphasis on scientific publications and the introduction of a dataset tailored for this purpose contribute to the originality. Exploring tool-based OOS testing and highlighting the interplay between base model capabilities, reasoning strategies, and tool dependencies is a worthwhile addition.

*   **Significance:**  The work addresses a critical issue: the reliability and reproducibility of LLM agents operating with external knowledge sources. Scientific knowledge is intrinsically linked to time, and the paper clearly demonstrates how the temporal context of information accessed through tools can significantly impact agent performance. This is important because of the increasing use of LLMs as assistants in scientific and professional domains. The paper highlights potential vulnerabilities that can arise from outdated or biased information retrieval and the need for robust evaluation methodologies that account for these temporal factors. It highlights the problems in using the tool that relies on a search engine that may produce different results based on the date.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The experimental setup is well-defined. The use of ReAct architecture coupled with DCT and a specific completion task allows for controlled investigation of the impact of date constraints.

    *   The inclusion of different LLM models (GPT-3.5, GPT-4) offers insights into how model capabilities interact with tool dependency.

    *   The use of Rouge-L and STS metrics provides a combination of verbatim and semantic similarity assessment.

    *   The paper clearly defines the research questions and addresses them methodically.

    *   The example reasoning traces provide valuable qualitative insights into agent behavior.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dataset, while contributing, is relatively small. Expanding the dataset, and potentially including the nature of change in the scientific breakthroughs, would strengthen the results.

    *   The reliance on Google Search API introduces a dependency on a specific search engine and its algorithms. The temporal effects might be different on different search engines. This may introduce bias in the observations.

    *   The analysis could benefit from exploring more sophisticated methods for temporal tool selection, potentially employing techniques from time series forecasting or causal inference.

    * The work doesn't deeply explore the *causes* of the temporal degradation but focuses primarily on *demonstrating* its existence and how to mitigate it. Further analysis should include a more robust justification on the changes in scientific knowledge and why that is affected, since scientific knowledge doesn't degrade so easily compared to factual knowledge of temporal facts.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the way LLM agents are evaluated, advocating for a more dynamic, tool-aware assessment that considers the temporal context. It also emphasizes the need for more robust agent designs that can mitigate the risks associated with outdated or biased information from external sources.

*   **Justification of Score:**

    The paper presents a well-executed investigation into an important, but not entirely novel, area. While the dataset and the specific focus on scientific abstract completion are valuable contributions, the paper's primary strength lies in its clear demonstration of how temporal effects can impact agent performance. The use of several LLMs and comparing the differences in their responses supports the argument that these agents can be improved with a careful selection of the LLMs. The limited dataset and the narrow focus of the experiment limits the impact of the work. Hence, a moderate score is provided.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### MASTER: Multimodal Segmentation with Text Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04199v1)
- **Authors**: Fuyang Liu, Shun Lu, Jilin Mei, Yu Hu
- **Abstract**: RGB-Thermal fusion is a potential solution for various weather and light conditions in challenging scenarios. However, plenty of studies focus on designing complex modules to fuse different modalities. With the widespread application of large language models (LLMs), valuable information can be more effectively extracted from natural language. Therefore, we aim to leverage the advantages of large language models to design a structurally simple and highly adaptable multimodal fusion model architecture. We proposed MultimodAl Segmentation with TExt PRompts (MASTER) architecture, which integrates LLM into the fusion of RGB-Thermal multimodal data and allows complex query text to participate in the fusion process. Our model utilizes a dual-path structure to extract information from different modalities of images. Additionally, we employ LLM as the core module for multimodal fusion, enabling the model to generate learnable codebook tokens from RGB, thermal images, and textual information. A lightweight image decoder is used to obtain semantic segmentation results. The proposed MASTER performs exceptionally well in benchmark tests across various automated driving scenarios, yielding promising results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MASTER (Multimodal Segmentation with Text Prompts), a novel architecture for RGB-Thermal image segmentation in autonomous driving scenarios. MASTER leverages a large language model (LLM) to fuse information from RGB images, thermal images, and textual prompts. The architecture consists of a dual-path Vision Transformer (ViT) to extract image features, an LLM to generate learnable codebook tokens from the different modalities, and a lightweight image decoder for semantic segmentation. The authors claim state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance on the MFNet benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in integrating an LLM into the RGB-Thermal fusion process. Using LLMs for multimodal fusion, especially with text prompts guiding the segmentation, is a relatively unexplored area in this specific domain.

    *   **Performance:** The authors report SOTA results on the MFNet dataset, suggesting the approach's effectiveness in fusing multimodal data.

    *   **Simplicity and Adaptability:** The authors emphasize the structural simplicity and high adaptability of the proposed fusion model. This is an advantage over existing methods that often use complex, asymmetric fusion modules.

    *   **Codebook Learning:** The use of learnable codebook tokens generated by the LLM is a clever way to represent the fused information from different modalities.
    *   **Addressing Specific Limitations of RGB-T fusion:** The paper directly confronts issues like poor performance of other methods on small object segmentation and noisy outputs in adverse conditions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty Beyond LLM Integration:** While the LLM integration is novel, the rest of the architecture (ViT encoders, decoder) is fairly standard. The degree of architectural innovation beyond the LLM is limited.
    *   **Dependency on LLM Performance:** The performance of MASTER is highly dependent on the LLM's capabilities. While LLMs are powerful, they can be computationally expensive and prone to errors or biases.
    *   **Benchmark Reliance:** The paper relies heavily on the MFNet dataset. A more robust evaluation across multiple datasets and scenarios would strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   **Limited Detail in Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative analysis relies solely on visual comparison. A more in-depth analysis of failure cases and the LLM's reasoning process would be valuable.

    *   **No Comparative Evaluation in Terms of Inference Time:** The paper shows superior performance with MASTER, but does not include any quantitative comparison of inference time with existing methods. With the integration of computationally expensive large language models, the increase in inference time might affect the applicability in real-time situations.
    *   **Ablation Study:** It would be nice to include an ablation study to demonstrate the contribution of different modalities and design choices of LLM integration.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper opens up a new avenue for RGB-Thermal fusion by leveraging the power of LLMs. It shows that LLMs can be effectively used to guide the segmentation process with text prompts, potentially leading to more robust and adaptable systems.
    *   If the approach proves to be computationally feasible, it could be a valuable tool for autonomous driving and other applications that require reliable scene understanding in challenging conditions.
    *   The use of text prompts for guiding segmentation is a promising direction for interactive and controllable image understanding.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*   The novelty of integrating LLMs into the RGB-Thermal fusion pipeline and using text prompts is significant. The reported SOTA performance on MFNet is also a positive indicator.
*   However, the paper's reliance on a single benchmark, the limited architectural innovation beyond the LLM integration, and lack of a comprehensive ablation study and computational expense, weakens the overall contribution.
*   The paper shows promise and warrants further research, but it is not a groundbreaking contribution that will revolutionize the field. A more thorough evaluation and further exploration of the computational aspects are needed.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Knowledge-Decoupled Synergetic Learning: An MLLM based Collaborative Approach to Few-shot Multimodal Dialogue Intention Recognition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.04201v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Chen, Yu Zhang, Hongfei Ye, Ziyi Huang, Hongyang Chen
- **Abstract**: Few-shot multimodal dialogue intention recognition is a critical challenge in the e-commerce domainn. Previous methods have primarily enhanced model classification capabilities through post-training techniques. However, our analysis reveals that training for few-shot multimodal dialogue intention recognition involves two interconnected tasks, leading to a seesaw effect in multi-task learning. This phenomenon is attributed to knowledge interference stemming from the superposition of weight matrix updates during the training process. To address these challenges, we propose Knowledge-Decoupled Synergetic Learning (KDSL), which mitigates these issues by utilizing smaller models to transform knowledge into interpretable rules, while applying the post-training of larger models. By facilitating collaboration between the large and small multimodal large language models for prediction, our approach demonstrates significant improvements. Notably, we achieve outstanding results on two real Taobao datasets, with enhancements of 6.37\% and 6.28\% in online weighted F1 scores compared to the state-of-the-art method, thereby validating the efficacy of our framework.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of few-shot multimodal dialogue intention recognition in e-commerce. It identifies a "seesaw effect" in multi-task learning for this problem, attributing it to knowledge interference during weight matrix updates in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The authors propose Knowledge-Decoupled Synergetic Learning (KDSL), a framework that uses a smaller MLLM to generate interpretable rules via Monte Carlo tree search, which are then combined with a fine-tuned larger MLLM for collaborative prediction. Experiments on Taobao datasets show significant improvements compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel approach to mitigating the interference issues in multi-task learning for multimodal dialogue systems. The idea of decoupling knowledge by using a smaller model for rule generation and a larger model for pattern recognition is interesting. Employing MCTS within an MLLM for rule generation, particularly with self-assessment to streamline search efficiency, represents a valuable technical contribution. However, the idea of knowledge distillation from large models to rule based systems has been proposed before, so novelty is partially incremental.

*   **Significance:** The problem of few-shot learning in multimodal dialogue, especially in the e-commerce domain, is highly relevant. The identified "seesaw effect" and its explanation through knowledge interference provide valuable insights into the challenges of training MLLMs for such tasks. The proposed KDSL framework demonstrates promising results, suggesting a practical approach to improve performance. The improvements of 6.37% and 6.28% on online weighted F1 scores are significant in a competitive e-commerce environment. Also, the detailed ablations studies help to understand the individual components and their interactions. However, the paper needs to address whether the 6.37% and 6.28% improvements are statistically significant.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-articulated explanation of the "seesaw effect" and its underlying cause.
    *   Novel framework (KDSL) with a clear methodology.
    *   Strong experimental results on real-world datasets.
    *   Detailed ablation studies providing insights into component contributions.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    * The novelty is incremental, as the underlying ideas (knowledge distillation, rule based systems for domain specific knowledge, etc.) are not entirely novel.
    *   Limited discussion of computational costs and scalability of the proposed framework (especially MCTS).
